% =====================================================================
\section{The celestial and twistor continuum route}\label{sec:celestial}
% =====================================================================
The previous section identified the seam's boundary $2$-sphere with the celestial sphere of null
directions ($\mathrm{M\ddot{o}b}(\hat{\mathbb C})\cong\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb C)\cong\mathrm{SO}^+(3,1)$,
clock $=$ order-$4$ M\"obius map \veri{v180\_clock\_is\_mobius.py}). Taking that identification seriously
and reading the seam through the celestial-holography lens (the celestial-chiral-algebra construction of
Bittleston--Homans--Sharma on ALE spaces; Costello's twistorial SDYM) produces a coherent
thirty-two-step
story --- the narrative arc from the $\mu_4$ clock to the $(E_8)_1$ boundary shadow --- executed as
work packages WP1--WP5d (both WP5d stages) plus the WP5e $\alpha$, $\beta$,
$\gamma$, $\delta_1$, $\delta_2$, $\varepsilon_2$ and $\varepsilon_1$ stages, the three back-reaction
milestones M1--M3, the measure decision (Step 21) and the constructive $w_m$ derivation
(Step 24) of the research contract
\texttt{CELEST.SEAM.01}, plus the RP-blindness decision on the alignment bit (Step 22) with its
twist-state completion (Step 26) with its twist-class definition (Step 29)
and the interacting FK-toy scoreboard extension (Step 30) with the straddle-cone
selection law (Step 31), the thermal-seam
third leg of $c_3$ (Step 27) with the silver-eigenvalue demystification (Step 28), and the
$\alpha$, $\beta_1$, $\beta_2$ and $\beta_3$ stages of the OS twistor bridge
\texttt{WOIT.OS.TWISTOR.01} (Steps 20, 23, 25 and 32), whose
full technical statement, kill tests and typing fence live in \texttt{tfpt\_research\_contracts}. Every
step below is exact machine arithmetic \mE\ plus explicitly named identifications \mC; the route is a
\emph{programme} \mO, not a claim, and nothing in it moves \texttt{SEAM.EQUIV.01}. The name of the
route is deliberately \emph{structural} (``celestial and twistor continuum route'', matching the
contract heading): what the route supplies is a candidate \emph{continuum} construction on the twistor
side, and the object diagram, the exact group-extension definition ($\Gamma_{\mathrm{ALE}}$ vs the
normalising clock $\langle\rho\rangle$) and the $A_3$ role table that fence its semantics live in the
contract (\texttt{tfpt\_research\_contracts}, \texttt{CELEST.SEAM.01}/\texttt{WOIT.OS.TWISTOR.01}).

\par\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1 --- the setup: the $\mu_4$ glue is a flat $\Z_4$ monodromy on the
$A_3$ ALE space \mE.} The $E_8$ $\mu_4$-glue grading
$240=52{+}64{+}60{+}64$ (\vref{v128}) is \emph{inner}: $h=(2,2,2,2,2;0^4)$ reads the glue class mod~$4$
on all $240$ roots, so the glue is $\operatorname{Ad}(\exp(2\pi ih/4))$ --- a flat $\Z_4$ monodromy in
the Kronheimer--Nakajima sense on the $A_3$ ALE orbifold $\mathbb C^2/\Z_4$ (which \emph{is} the $A_3$
singularity $XY=Z^4$, exactly). The glue-equivariant SDYM$(E_8)$ sector closes as a Lie algebra with
graded dimensions $60(d{+}1)/64(d{+}1)$ --- possible \emph{only} because
$\dim\mathfrak g_j=(60,64,60,64)$ --- with zero modes $=$ the carrier $D_5{\oplus}A_3{+}$Cartan $=60$,
and the four glue-sector characters sum \emph{exactly} to the $(E_8)_1$ character
$\chi=1{+}248q{+}4124q^2{+}34752q^3$ (\vref{v377}). The critical correction: the $A_3$ deck induces on
the celestial sphere only the sheet flip $z\mapsto-z$; the order-$4$ clock $z\mapsto iz$ is realised as
the K\"ahler $U(2)$ phase $\operatorname{diag}(i,1)$, and \textbf{clock$^2=$ deck} exactly (spin bridge
$\Z_8$, $8=2|\mu_4|$ --- the $\cthree=1/(8\pi)$ seam winding integer).
\veri{v492\_celestial\_z4\_orbifold.py} That spin bookkeeping is now \emph{forced} on the seam
fermions: the NS implementer of the deck satisfies $U^2=(-1)^F$ exactly (nonsplit $\Z_4$ extension,
no phase choice escapes it), and the quarter-shift lifts to a canonical $\Z_8$ tower with
$V^2\propto U$, $V^4\propto(-1)^F$ --- ``$8=2|\mu_4|$'' fermionically explained; the R-sector
control splits ($U_R^2=+1$), so the forcing rests exactly on the established bounding spin
structure. \veri{v506\_seam\_clock\_rigidity.py}

\par\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2 --- the deformation: $a_0$ is a pure scale and the clock is the
monodromy \mE.} Clock-invariance selects from the
versal $A_3$ family exactly the one-parameter slice $XY=Z^4{+}a_0$, and $a_0$ carries \emph{no} shape
modulus: the binary-quartic invariants are $I=12a_0$, $J=0$ \emph{identically}, so $j=1728$ and the
$\tau{=}i$ pillowcase (\vref{v168}/\vref{v214}) is frozen for every $a_0$. The clock acts on $H_2$ as a
\emph{Coxeter element} of $W(A_3)$ (eigenvalues $\{i,-1,-i\}$ $=$ the $A_3$ exponents,
$h(A_3){=}4{=}|\mu_4|$) and \emph{is} the Picard--Lefschetz monodromy of the family; the deformed-algebra
pattern transfers $\Z_2\to\Z_4$ grade-conserving (no $\mu_4$-sector leak).
\veri{v493\_celestial\_wp2\_clock\_deformation.py} The converse is now tested \emph{light}: the free
field alone does \emph{not} select $\tau=i$ (the global $\det{}'$ winner is the hexagonal torus, whose
$\Z_6$ carries no order-$4$ element) --- the square is pinned by the clock's \emph{rigidity} (the raw
DtN is mod-$4$ block-diagonal \emph{iff} $\tau=i$), and this order-$4$ clock is the \emph{same} carrier
datum as the P2 cusp class (\vref{v499}): one common residual, not two
(\veri{v503\_qgeo\_emergence\_light.py}; the v215 deferred emergence test, light version --- no marker
moves, \texttt{QGEO.REALIZE.01} stays \mC/\mO). That common residual is now reduced to \emph{one bit}:
the deck $z\mapsto-z$ has exactly two M\"obius square roots ($z\mapsto\pm iz$), both automatically of
order $4$, and a marks-preserving root exists \emph{iff} the deck is the \emph{central} involution of
the mark-stabiliser $D_4$ --- given that alignment bit, order, uniqueness up to $\operatorname{Aut}(\Z_4)$,
the free $4$-cycle and $\tau=i$ (scale free) are all theorems; harmonicity alone is \emph{not} enough
on bare mark data (exact counterexample --- though on free-deck circles it becomes sufficient, see
below), and the fermionic $\Z_4$ forces the \emph{order}, not the alignment
(\veri{v506\_seam\_clock\_rigidity.py}, \texttt{SEAM.CLOCK.RIGIDITY.01}; markers unchanged). The bit
itself has now survived its tautology attack: marks and deck \emph{do} share one torus origin ---
the marks are the four half-periods $\Lambda/2\Lambda$ of the seam double, and \emph{every}
mark-free collar deck is a half-period translation (in all three stabiliser types the freely-acting
involutions are exactly the three $\sigma_c$), so the deck's \emph{class} is derived --- yet the
origin does not force the alignment: the marks-preserving root exists iff the deck pairs separate
harmonically ($\lambda\in\{-1,2,\tfrac12\}$, one value per half-period), the aligned half-period is
the CM-fixed one $c^*=(1{+}\tau)/2$ (the clock's curve lift needs the unit $i$ of $\Z[i]$, while
half-period decks lift rationally), and the silver counterexample sits \emph{in-family} as
``$\tau=i$ with the deck a non-CM-fixed half-period''. The bit is thereby an equivalence
\emph{tetrad} --- clock $\iff$ ($\tau{=}i$ and deck $=$ CM-fixed half-period) $\iff$ (Stab $=D_4$
and deck central) $\iff$ harmonic deck pairs $\iff$ \emph{nonsplit} NS Fock lift --- with a physical
face: the central arrangement carries the nontrivial Fidkowski--Kitaev-type extension class
($U^2=(-1)^F$), the edge arrangement splits ($U_e^2=+1$, zero roots, no $\Z_8$ tower); only the
\emph{position} (which half-period) stays the carrier input
(\veri{v507\_seam\_bit\_origin.py}, \texttt{SEAM.BIT.ORIGIN.01}; no marker moves). And that position
half is now \emph{topology}: the collar deck is the deck transformation of a \emph{covering} (the
$\Z_2$ seam of the M\"obius double), and covering decks act freely --- on the seam circle the unique
free involution is the antipode ($\operatorname{Aut}(C_{16})=D_{16}$, complete census; every
reflection has two circle fixed points, and its quotient is an \emph{interval}, breaking the
closed-circle $6\pi$ budget), the NS Fock lift is nonsplit \emph{iff} the deck is free (all $17$
involutions in exact $\mathrm{Cl}(16)$), and the edge/silver arrangement --- whose mark-fixing real
structure has its fix circle \emph{through} the deck poles --- is the restriction of the
\emph{branched} pillowcase involution, not a covering deck: excluded. On free-deck circles
``harmonic'' already solves to $b=\pm i$, so the alignment bit reduces from ``harmonic \emph{and}
central'' to the square-modulus datum $\tau=i$ alone --- the position half is topology, the modulus
half stays the one carrier input
(\veri{v510\_seam\_bit\_freedom.py}, \texttt{SEAM.BIT.FREEDOM.01}; markers unchanged). The modulus
half now has a sharp \emph{discrete} face: on the free seam circle the deck-invariant $4$-mark
configurations form the one-parameter family $M(\delta)=\{\pm1,\pm e^{i\delta}\}$, and \emph{bare}
mark-transitivity is automatic for every $\delta$ (the pair-exchanging $V_4$; all undirected
per-mark data are identical, so no local jet sees the bit --- ``transitivity from
\vref{v216}-locality'' is falsified) --- what selects $\delta=\pi/2\iff\tau=i$ is exactly
\emph{flag} transitivity (marks \emph{and} their two sides indistinguishable, the $V_4\to D_4$
symmetry lift), welded into a $13$-fold exact equivalence web (clock rotation, mark mirror,
odd-doublet split $(2/\pi)\ln\cot(\delta/2)$, arc-Laplacian degeneracy, the \vref{v503} mod-$4$
indicator in closed form, $\rho$-twist existence, cusp-$4$-cycle implementability, harmonicity,
$j=1728$, and --- since Step 22 --- the $D_4$ closure of the OS-reflection group); the carrier
input thereby reduces from a continuous modulus to \emph{one discrete
symmetry-lift bit} whose negation is concretely measurable
(\veri{v512\_seam\_tau\_flag.py}, \texttt{SEAM.TAU.FLAG.01}; markers unchanged).

\par\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3 --- consistency, honestly demoted \mE/\mC.}
$E_8$ is on Costello's axionic-consistency list (no

\par\medskip\noindent\textbf{The Woit bridge: chiral Wick rotation and the real structure \mO.}
One \emph{external} programme is close enough in shape to deserve a named paragraph --- named
precisely so that proximity is not mistaken for confirmation. Woit's \emph{Euclidean Twistor
Unification} (\texttt{arXiv:2104.05099}) starts from the observation that the usual Wick rotation is
problematic for \emph{purely chiral} theories (the Euclidean continuation of a chiral fermion is not
a chiral fermion; $SO(4)\cong SU(2){\times}SU(2)$ splits what $SO^+(3,1)$ holds together), and
proposes the counter-move: formulate the theory \emph{Euclidean-holomorphically on projective twistor
space} $\mathbb{PT}$, and reconstruct the Lorentzian theory from a conjugation structure / boundary
values --- the real form is \emph{recovered}, not assumed. TFPT's celestial/twistor route has,
independently, assembled the very ingredients such a reconstruction needs: reflection positivity of
the seam collar (\vref{v379}; the Osterwalder--Schrader axiom, machine-witnessed), the OS
reconstruction step itself (the cited AMT/OS selector in \texttt{FORM.SEAM.MMST.01}), the order-$4$
clock $\rho=\operatorname{diag}(i,1)$ acting on the twistor sphere (\vref{v492}), and the seam
reflection (Step 20 makes the referent precise: the seam-circle \emph{reflection}, not the
deck/covering involution --- the deck, whose freeness is topology \vref{v510}, carries the
$(-1)^F$ Kramers class instead).
What is \emph{missing} is stated exactly: a \emph{global anti-linear map}
$\Theta:\mathcal A(\mathbb{PT}/\Gamma)\to\mathcal A(\mathbb{PT}/\Gamma)$ with $\Theta^2=1$ and
$\Theta\rho\Theta=\rho^{-1}$ on the \emph{interacting} open$+$closed twistorial algebra, together
with reflection positivity of the interacting BCOV$+$SDYM functional --- the two inputs of the new
central contract \texttt{WOIT.OS.TWISTOR.01} in \texttt{tfpt\_research\_contracts}. The
$\alpha$-stage status (Step 20, \vref{v519}): \emph{the missing global object now exists at the
free level} --- $\Theta$ with $\Theta^2=+1$ and $\Theta\rho\Theta=\rho^{-1}$ on all four levels

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.4em]

  $E_8$ closure (two independent Lie-theory conditions); the integer ``$8$'' in $\cthree$ is the carrier
  Coxeter $=$ $E_8$ rank; the discrete data closes a loop. The earlier landscape scan confirms
  $\gcar=5$ is the \emph{unique} value at which all hierarchies/family/gauge close --- so the fixed point
  is unique, not one of many. \textbf{This uniqueness is now machine-formalised \mE\ in Lean 4} (Theorem A,
  \texttt{lean4-carrier-rigidity}): the division-free Pascal condition $2^{g}=g^2+g+2$ has the \emph{unique}
  solution $g=5$ (\texttt{carrier\_rank\_pascal\_unique}; growth lemma for $g\ge6$; $\Nfam=(2^{4}{-}1)/5=3$,
  $g{+}\Nfam=8$), audited to use only the standard kernel axioms. The 2026-06-11 hardening round adds two
  formal modules: \texttt{AnchorLadder.lean} (the anchor power-sum law $p_n(a)=2+2^n$ for \emph{all} $n$,
  the ladder atoms $(3,4,6,10)$, $240/8/248$, and the binary ladder $p_{n+1}{-}p_n=2^n$ as general
  theorems) and \texttt{PascalLadder.lean} (the odd-midpoint ladder identity

  uniqueness of the $K{=}2$ carrier case) --- the solves-side of the Pascal Ladder Theorem
  (\veri{v108\_pascal\_ladder.py}) is thereby \mE.
\item \textbf{What this is \emph{not}:} a derivation from zero inputs. A self-consistency loop is
  consistent \emph{at} a value; uniqueness is what makes it predictive (and that is supplied by the
  landscape scan). The continuous $\pi$ is not produced by the loop --- it is the Möbius/boundary

\item \textbf{Conceptual gain:} the theory's foundation tightens from ``two free axioms'' to ``\emph{the}
  $E_8$-closure fixed point $+$ the geometric $\pi$.'' That is the Möbius self-consistency you intuited:

\begin{winbox}[Item 1b closed: the explicit solar-angle Majorana texture \mE]
The last open SM angle is now realised by an \emph{explicit} texture, not a fitted number. Take the
$\mu\tau$-symmetric Majorana matrix (tri-bimaximal at $\varepsilon{=}0$)
\begin{equation}
  M_\nu(\varepsilon)=\begin{pmatrix}-\eta(\varepsilon)&1&1\\ 1&1&0\\ 1&0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
  \eta(\varepsilon)=\frac{2\sqrt2}{\tan2\theta_{12}}-1,\quad
  \sin^2\theta_{12}=\tfrac13(1-2\varepsilon).
\end{equation}
$\mu\tau$-symmetry forces $\theta_{23}=45^\circ$ and $\theta_{13}=0$ \emph{exactly}; diagonalisation at the
TFPT solar deviation $\varepsilon=\tfrac34\phiz$ gives
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{\ \sin^2\theta_{12}=\tfrac13-\tfrac{\phiz}{2}=0.306747,\quad
  \theta_{23}=45^\circ,\quad \theta_{13}=0\ }\quad\mE
\end{equation}
\textbf{Honest residual} (\veri{v9\_neutrino\_texture.py}). The texture and the angle are exact. The seam identification is
$\varepsilon=\tfrac34\phiz=\tfrac34\bigl(\tfrac1{6\pi}+\tfrac3{256\pi^4}\bigr)=\cthree+\tfrac{9}{1024\pi^4}$:
the \emph{leading geometric term is exactly} $\cthree=\tfrac1{8\pi}$, and the $0.23\%$ residual is purely the
$\phiz$ seed tail (document~2). So $\varepsilon=\cthree$ holds at leading order; the full
$\varepsilon=\tfrac34\phiz$ is what enters the texture. The reactor angle $\theta_{13}$
($\sin^2\theta_{13}=e^{-5/6}\phiz$, document~2) enters through a separate $\mu\tau$-breaking term and is not
part of this minimal solar texture. \textbf{Scope:} this realises the TFPT solar shift in the
$\mu\tau$-symmetric \emph{limit}; it is \emph{not} the full PMNS matrix. The $\theta_{23}{=}45^\circ$ value
is the symmetric-limit centre, \emph{not} an octant prediction --- current global fits (NuFIT~6.0) leave
the octant unresolved --- and the reactor angle plus the combined matrix require the separately stated
$\mu\tau$-breaking channel.
\end{winbox}

\section{Following the $SU(9)$ lead: cross-slice consistency}
% =====================================================================
The one scan lead worth following is $A_8=SU(9)\supset SU(5)\times SU(4)$. Splitting $9=(5,1)\oplus(1,4)$
the $E_8$ branching $248=80\oplus84\oplus\overline{84}$ decomposes \emph{exactly} as:

\begin{center}\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}llY@{}}
\toprule
piece & $SU(5)\times SU(4)$ content & reading \\
\midrule
$80$ (adj) & $(24,1){+}(1,15){+}(5,\bar4){+}(\bar5,4){+}(1,1)$ & $SU(5)_{\rm GUT}$ $+$ $SU(4)_{\rm fam}$ gauge \\
$84=\Lambda^3 9$ & $(10,1){+}(10,4){+}(5,6){+}(1,4)$ & the SM $\mathbf{10}$ as family-singlet $+$ family-$\mathbf4$ \\
$\overline{84}$ & $(\overline{10},1){+}(\overline{10},\bar4){+}(\bar5,6){+}(1,\bar4)$ & the conjugate matter \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

\noindent (Dimension checks: $24{+}15{+}20{+}20{+}1=80$; $10{+}40{+}30{+}4=84$; total $248$.)

\begin{winbox}[The genuine content: $A_8$ and $D_5{\times}A_3$ agree on the family \mE]
The $SU(4)$ here is \emph{the same} $A_3$ family group as in the construction slice $D_5\times A_3$ --- the
four punctures $\mu_4$ of $\PP^1\setminus\mu_4$. The $SU(5)$ is the Georgi--Glashow GUT sitting inside the
carrier $SO(10)=D_5$ ($SU(5)\subset SO(10)$). So the two maximal slices are \emph{consistent}: both read
the family as $SU(4){=}A_3$, and $A_8$ additionally exposes the $SU(5)$ matter content --- the SM
$\mathbf{10}$ appears as $(10,1)\oplus(10,4)$, i.e.\ a family-singlet $\mathbf{10}$ plus a family-$\mathbf4$,
where the $\mathbf4$ of $A_3$ is exactly TFPT's ``$4=\Nfam+1$'' (three families $+$ the $\mu_4$ direction).
\end{winbox}

\begin{gapbox}[Honest limit: chirality is still the index, not $E_8$]
$84\oplus\overline{84}$ is \emph{vectorlike} ($10\oplus\overline{10}$ etc.): $E_8$ being non-chiral, the
slice cannot by itself produce three \emph{chiral} generations. The chirality is the boundary index
$\operatorname{Ind}(D^+_{\rm fam})=3$ --- the \emph{same} input TFPT already uses. So $SU(9)$ is a
consistent re-reading (matter in $SU(5)$, family in $A_3$), not an independent derivation of $\Nfam=3$.
Each $SU(5)$ pair $(\mathbf{10}+\bar{\mathbf5})$ is anomaly-free, so the embedding is clean. \mC
\end{gapbox}
